The CPU core is heavily parametrizable and well suited for\n10: embedded control applications. Ibex is being extensively verified and has\n11: seen multiple tape-outs. Ibex supports the Integer (I) or Embedded (E),\n12: Integer Multiplication and Division (M), Compressed (C), and B (Bit\n13: Manipulation) extensions.\n14: \n15: <p align=\"center\"><img src=\"doc/03_reference/images/blockdiagram.svg\" width=\"650\"></p>\n16: \n17: Ibex was initially developed as part of the [PULP platform](https://www.pulp-platform.org)\n18: under the name [\"Zero-riscy\"](https://doi.org/10.1109/PATMOS.2017.8106976), and has been\n19: contributed to [lowRISC](https://www.lowrisc.org) who maintains it and develops it further. It is\n20: under active development.\n21: \n22: ## Configuration\n23: \n24: Ibex offers several configuration parameters to meet the needs of various application scenarios.\n25: The options include different choices for the architecture of the multiplier unit, as well as a range of performance and security features.\n26: The table below indicates performance, area and verification status for a few selected configurations.\n27: These are configurations on which lowRISC is focusing for performance evaluation and design verification (see [supported configs](ibex_configs.yaml)).\n28: \n29: | Config | \"micro\" | \"small\" | \"maxperf\" | \"maxperf-pmp-bmfull\" |\n30: | ------ | ------- | --------| ----------| -------------------- |\n31: | Features | RV32EC | RV32IMC, 3 cycle mult | RV32IMC, 1 cycle mult, Branch target ALU, Writeback stage | RV32IMCB, 1 cycle mult, Branch target ALU, Writeback stage, 16 PMP regions |\n32: | Performance (CoreMark/MHz) | 0.904 | 2.47 | 3.13 | 3.13 |\n33: | Area - Yosys (kGE) | 16.85 | 26.60 | 32.48 | 66.02 |\n34: | Area - Commercial (estimated kGE) | ~15 | ~24 | ~30 | ~61 |\n35: | Verification status | Red | Green | Green | Green |\n36: \n37: Notes:\n38: \n39: * Performance numbers are based on CoreMark running on the Ibex Simple System [platform](examples/simple_system/README.md).\n40:   Note that different ISAs (use of B and C extensions) give the best results for different configurations.\n41:   See the [Benchmarks README](examples/sw/benchmarks/README.md) for more information.\n42: * Yosys synthesis area numbers are based on the Ibex basic synthesis [flow](syn/README.md) using the latch-based register file.\n43: * Commercial synthesis area numbers are a rough estimate of what might be achievable with a commercial synthesis flow and technology library.\n44: * For comparison, the original \"Zero-riscy\" core yields an area of 23.14kGE using our Yosys synthesis flow.\n45: * Verification status is a rough guide to the overall maturity of a particular configuration.\n46:   Green indicates that verification is close to complete.\n47:   Amber indicates that some verification has been performed, but the configuration is still experimental.\n48:   Red indicates a configuration with minimal/no verification.\n49:   Users must make their own assessment of verification readiness for any tapeout.\n50: * v.1.0.0 of the RISC-V Bit-Manipulation Extension is supported as well as the remaining sub-extensions of draft v.0.93 of the bitmanip spec.\n51:   The latter are *not ratified* and there may be changes before ratification.\n52:   See [Standards Compliance](https://ibex-core.readthedocs.io/en/latest/01_overview/compliance.html) in the Ibex documentation for more information.\n53: \n54: ## Documentation\n55: \n56: The Ibex user manual can be\n57: [read online at ReadTheDocs](https://ibex-core.readthedocs.io/en/latest/). It is also contained in\n58: the `doc` folder of this repository.\n59: \n60: ## Examples\n61: \n62: The Ibex repository includes [Simple System](examples/simple_system/README.md).\n63: This is an intentionally simple integration of Ibex with a basic system that targets simulation.\n64: It is intended to provide an easy way to get bare metal binaries running on Ibex in simulation.\n65: \n66: A more complete example can be found in the [Ibex Demo System repository](https://github.com/lowrisc/ibex-demo-system).\n67: In particular it includes a integration of the [PULP RISC-V debug module](https://github.com/pulp-platform/riscv-dbg).\n68: It targets the [Arty A7 FPGA board from Digilent](https://digilent.com/shop/arty-a7-artix-7-fpga-development-board/) and supports debugging via OpenOCD and GDB over USB (no external JTAG probe required).\n69: The Ibex Demo System is maintained by lowRISC but is not an official part of Ibex.\n70: \n71: ## Contributing\n72: \n73: We highly appreciate community contributions. To ease our work of reviewing your contributions,\n74: please:\n75: \n76: * Create your own branch to commit your changes and then open a Pull Request.\n77: * Split large contributions into smaller commits addressing individual changes or bug fixes. Do not\n78:   mix unrelated changes into the same commit!\n79: * Write meaningful commit messages. For more information, please check out the [contribution\n80:   guide](https://github.com/lowrisc/ibex/blob/master/CONTRIBUTING.md).\n81: * If asked to modify your changes, do fixup your commits and rebase your branch to maintain a\n82:   clean history.\n83: \n84: When contributing SystemVerilog source code, please try to be consistent and adhere to [our Verilog\n85: coding style guide](https://github.com/lowRISC/style-guides/blob/master/VerilogCodingStyle.md).\n86: \n87: When contributing C or C++ source code, please try to adhere to [the OpenTitan C++ coding style\n88: guide](https://opentitan.org/book/doc/contributing/style_guides/c_cpp_coding_style.html).\n89: All C and C++ code should be formatted with clang-format before committing.\n90: Either run `clang-format -i filename.cc` or `git clang-format` on added files.\n91: \n92: To get started, please check out the [\"Good First Issue\"\n93:  list](https://github.com/lowrisc/ibex/issues?q=is%3Aissue+is%3Aopen+label%3A%22Good+First+Issue%22).\n94: \n95: ## Issues and Troubleshooting\n96: \n97: If you find any problems or issues with Ibex or the documentation, please check out the [issue\n98:  tracker](https://github.com/lowrisc/ibex/issues) and create a new issue if your problem is\n99: not yet tracked.\n100: \n101: ## Questions?\n102: \n103: Do not hesitate to contact us, e.g., on our public [Ibex channel on\n104: Zulip](https://lowrisc.zulipchat.com/#narrow/stream/198227-ibex)!\n105: \n106: ## License\n107: \n108: Unless otherwise noted, everything in this repository is covered by the Apache\n109: License, Version 2.0 (see LICENSE for full text).\n110: \n111: ## Credits\n112: \n113: Many people have contributed to Ibex through the years. For interrupts and debug Ibex will take the relevant trap as soon as whatever instruction in ID\n1456:   // finishes or immediately if the ID stage is empty. The rvfi_ext interface provides the DV\n1457:   // environment with information about the irq/debug_req/nmi state that applies to a particular\n1458:   // instruction.\n1459:   //\n1460:   // When a irq/debug_req/nmi appears the ID stage will finish whatever instruction it is currently\n1461:   // executing (if any) then take the trap the cycle after that instruction leaves the ID stage. The\n1462:   // trap taken depends upon the state of irq/debug_req/nmi on that cycle. In the cycles following\n1463:   // that before the first instruction of the trap handler enters the ID stage the state of\n1464:   // irq/debug_req/nmi could change but this has no effect on the trap handler (e.g. a higher\n1465:   // priority interrupt might appear but this wouldn't stop the lower priority interrupt trap\n1466:   // handler executing first as it's already being fetched). To provide the DV environment with the\n1467:   // correct information for it to verify execution we need to capture the irq/debug_req/nmi state\n1468:   // the cycle the trap decision is made. Which the captured_X signals below do.\n1469:   //\n1470:   // The new_X signals take the raw irq/debug_req/nmi inputs and factor in the enable terms required\n1471:   // to determine if a trap will actually happen.\n1472:   //\n1473:   // These signals and the comment above are referred to in the documentation (cosim.rst).
